  Three stable sorts where upstream uses std::sort -- DELIBERATE

    std::sort is not stable. Script_column::order_grobs, Fingering_column's
    offset sort and New_fingering_engraver's Finger_tuple sort all feed
    decisions where the tie-break is visible: which fingering goes up and which
    goes down when a chord is split, and which of two flush fingerings moves.
    An unstable tie there is a coin flip a second implementation cannot
    reproduce, so the port sorts stably. (scm_stable_sort_x, which
    Script_column::order_grobs actually calls, is stable upstream too.)

  System::get_neighboring_staff skips hara-kiri suicide -- SAME AS vertical
  organization's

    Upstream calls Hara_kiri_group_spanner::consider_suicide on each candidate.
    That file is line breaking's and unported, exactly as
    staff-grouper-interface.cc
    already records. Skipping it can only leave a staff ALIVE that upstream
    would have killed, so the answer is never a staff upstream would not have
    considered. REVISIT WITH LINE BREAKING.

  Spanner::find_broken_piece guards a null first-piece system

    Upstream reads broken_intos_.front ()->get_system ()->get_rank () with no
    check, which is safe there because line breaking has always run. The port is
    asked before line breaking and page layout land, so the guard answers
    null instead of
    dereferencing. Same treatment as the slur guard: REMOVE AND RE-MEASURE
    once line breaking runs.

  Line_spanner::calc_bound_info's assert

    Upstream asserts extreme_bound_groups_there[dir] is non-null. An assert is
    compiled out of a release build, so upstream does not check it either; the
    port raises nothing and takes the same branch.


  DEFECTS FOUND IN ALREADY-PORTED CODE

  1. lily-guile.cc's ly_assoc -- assq COMPARED BOXED NUMBERS BY REFERENCE

     THE BIGGEST OF THEM, and it was silent. Guile fixnums, booleans and
     characters are IMMEDIATES, not heap objects, so (eq? 1 1) is true and
     (assq 1 '((1 . a))) finds its entry. SchemeUtilities.Assq compared with
     ReferenceEquals, under which two boxed longs of the same value are NEVER
     equal -- so every numerically-keyed alist lookup in the engine silently
     missed. It now compares with LilyScheme's own ReferenceComparer, which
     already had the right semantics.

     Found through Ottava_spanner_engraver: ottavationMarkups is keyed by octave
     count, so every \ottava warned "Could not find ottavation markup" and set
     the bracket's text to the empty string. Fenced by Epg14Tests.

  2. lily-guile.cc's value_type_check -- A PROCEDURE IS ALWAYS A VALID GROB
     PROPERTY

     The same function, a second gap. For `backend-type?' alone, upstream
     answers true for ANY procedure, because a grob property holding a callback
     is not the value the property will have -- it is the function that computes
     it, and it can only be checked once it has run. An unpure-pure container is
     checked by recursing into both halves. The port implemented neither, so
     `\override Hairpin.stencil = #flared-hairpin' -- ordinary LilyPond -- was
     REFUSED and the override never reached the grob.

     The bars/meter group had already fixed this function's FIRST
     short-circuit ('(), #f and
     *unspecified* always succeed). This is the next one down.

  3. grob.hh's find_broken_piece -- DECLARED, DEFINED THREE TIMES, NEVER CARRIED

     Declared virtual in grob.hh and defined in grob.cc, item.cc and spanner.cc,
     all three of which the ledger has called `ported' from the start. Nothing
     asked
     until Line_spanner::calc_bound_info needed to follow a cross-staff
     glissando's bound onto another system.

  4. pitch.cc's set_middle_C -- never carried. \ottava shifts middle C by an
     octave through it.

  5. system.cc's get_neighboring_staff -- never carried.
     Hairpin::broken_bound_padding is its only caller.

  6. grob.cc's robust_relative_pure_y_extent -- never carried, though its
     sibling robust_relative_extent was. Balloon_interface::pure_height is its
     only caller.

  7. nested-property.cc's set_nested_property -- the BODY existed, written out
     inline inside ly:grob-set-nested-property!. Tweak_engraver needs the
     function, so the binding now calls it and there is one implementation.

  Two general helpers also came home to lily-guile.cc's file
  (Objects/SchemeUtilities.cs): ly_assoc_get, which Stem had a private copy of,
  and ly_append, which Stencil and GlobalContext each had one of.


  BINDINGS PULLED FORWARD FROM THE LONG TAIL -- FORCED, NOT CHOSEN

  The scripts group's FIRST sweep regressed 46 files, and the cause was the
  group making
  progress: with ly:line-spanner::print ported, every trill spanner, text
  spanner and glissando produced a real stencil for the first time, so the
  skyline machinery ran on it -- and ly:skylines-for-stencil, which
  scm/define-grobs.scm names on those grobs, was still a stub. This is the
  iterator group's
  pattern exactly: files stop producing EMPTY pages and reach real engraving,
  where they hit gaps that were always there.

  Taken: lily/skyline-scheme.cc WHOLE (11 bindings),
  lily/line-interface-scheme.cc WHOLE (1 binding, ly:line-interface::line, which
  scm/output-lib.scm's flared-hairpin calls directly), and five leaves of
  lily/stencil-scheme.cc -- ly:stencil-outline, ly:round-polygon, ly:bracket,
  ly:stencil-rotate-absolute, ly:length -- plus item-scheme.cc's
  ly:item-get-column.

  skyline-scheme.cc was taken WHOLE deliberately: implementing
  ly:skylines-for-stencil alone moved the failure straight on to
  ly:skyline-max-height, which the same scm/ code path calls one line later.
  Its ledger row and line-interface-scheme.cc's therefore change disposition to
  `ported' -- a *-scheme.cc file IS its binding surface, which
  EntryPointClosureTests fences. stencil-scheme.cc does NOT: five leaves landed
  and the rest did not.

  STILL OWED and now visible: ly:paper-column::print is demanded 124 times per
  sweep and is NOT killing any file (it is the debug column-number stencil). It
  is paper-column.cc's own MAKE_SCHEME_CALLBACK, so it belongs to whoever
  finishes that file's binding surface. Left for the long tail.


  THE STENCIL EXPRESSION WALK -- THE TEXT-INTERFACE FOLLOW-UP IS CLOSED

  The text-interface group left text stencils taking their extent BOX rather
  than their real ink,
  because the port's utf-8-string node carried nothing to walk: upstream's
  fourth element is the glyph string Pango shaped, and the port has no Pango.
  Walking such a node anyway would have silently OMITTED the text, and a skyline
  that omits ink is the one error direction that causes collisions -- so the box
  was taken, conservatively, and the scripts group was named as the owner to
  close it.

  Closed. TextFontMetric.TextStencil already resolves every code point through
  the D23 fallback chain to a FACE, a GLYPH INDEX and a SCALE while it measures
  the run; it now also emits that run into the fourth element as
  (combine-stencil (translate-stencil (ADVANCE . 0) (glyph-outline FACE INDEX
  SCALE)) ...), and StencilIntegral traces each glyph's charstrings through the
  same CffFont.AddOutlineToSkyline the music glyphs use.

  `glyph-outline' is NEW-IN-FAMILY and never leaves the utf-8-string node, which
  the SVG backend explicitly ignores -- upstream's SVG output sets real text and
  lets the viewer's font engine draw it -- so output parity is untouched and
  only skylines change. It exists because a music glyph is addressed by NAME
  through a font metric while a text glyph is addressed by INDEX through the
  face the chain resolved, and carries its own scale because the chain can
  change face mid-run.

  PURE VARIANTS are still unchanged: they answer the ordinary extents, pending
  unpure-pure-container.cc with line breaking.


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE THREE ROWS THE SCRIPTS/DYNAMICS GROUP LEFT OPEN
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The scripts group closed with the ratchet gate RED on three rows and left
  them for a
  ruling rather than re-baselining them. All three were diagnosed to root this
  session; two were defects and are FIXED, and the third is a dependency on
  work line breaking and page layout own and is recorded here rather than
  papered over.


  THE TOPLEVEL \layout BLOCK NEVER REACHED ANY CONTEXT (script-custom-definition)

  This is the largest of the three and it was NOT what the file's error message
  said. The reported failure was (zero? '()) inside
  horizontal-script::calc-staff-position, reading an unset `side-axis'. That is
  a SYMPTOM two layers down: side-axis is not a Script grob default -- 
  scm/define-grobs.scm says "'padding, 'side-axis set in script definitions" --
  so it can only arrive from the scriptDefinitions alist, and the file's alist
  was never reaching the engraver at all. The warning that gave it away was
  "do not know how to interpret articulation: segno-coda", six times.

  THE DEFECT, and it is a whole mechanism rather than one property: NO property
  operation written in a toplevel \layout block had ANY effect, for the entire
  life of the batch runner. Neither an \override nor a plain assignment.

  It survived this long because it splits exactly in half, and the visible half
  works. A \layout { \context { ... } } block's TRANSLATOR list was applied
  correctly -- \consists really does add an engraver -- because a translator
  list is read off the ContextDef the block built. Property operations are not:
  they are applied by Context_def::apply_default_property_operations against
  whatever definition find_context_def answers with, and that lookup goes
  through the OUTPUT DEF. Any probe written with \consists reports the block
  working.

  Root cause: parser.yy's toplevel_expression REBINDS the $defaultlayout
  IDENTIFIER to the definition a toplevel \layout builds; it does not mutate
  the one already there. Upstream therefore resolves the layout BY NAME, late --
  print-book-with (scm/lily-library.scm) does (ly:parser-lookup '$defaultlayout)
  at book-processing time and hands the answer to ly:book-process. BatchRunner
  captured LilyPondInit.DefaultLayout(), the cached init-layer OBJECT, before
  the parse, so it always engraved under the pristine layout and every toplevel
  \layout in the suite was silently discarded.

  Fixed by resolving the identifier where upstream resolves it: inside the book
  handler, after the parse, falling back to the cached object when the
  identifier names nothing. ScoreLayout's own preference is unchanged -- a
  score's own \layout block still wins -- and needs no change, because
  get_layout (lily-parser.cc) opens every \layout head by CLONING whatever
  $defaultlayout names at that moment, so a score block already inherits the
  toplevel one and can then override it.

  This is the text-interface group's $defaultpaper finding one identifier
  over, and it is the same
  lesson twice: these definitions are reached BY NAME and rebound wholesale, so
  a cached reference to the object is not a reference to the current value.
  Fenced by ToplevelLayoutEndToEndTests, whose every case is paired against the
  same operation written in the music -- a path that never went through
  $defaultlayout and so always worked.


  slashed-beam -- NOT FIXED, AND THE REASON IS UNPLACED COLUMNS

  Reported as "(min) called with zero arguments". Traced to
  beam::calc-right-extreme-vertical-count in scm/output-lib.scm, which filters
  a kneed beam's beam-segments down to those touching the right end of the beam
  and then does (apply min <their vertical counts>). The filter comes back
  EMPTY, so min is applied to '().

  Measured on the file's own kneed beams: the stems of the failing beams sit at
  only TWO distinct x-coordinates -- 1.2392 and 0.065 -- and they run BACKWARDS,
  later stems at smaller x. Four notes, two positions, wrong order. That makes
  the segments' horizontal intervals INVERTED, e.g. (1.1742 . 0.13), and an
  inverted interval is is_empty() by Interval's own definition, so
  Beam::calc_x_positions falls into its own "beams without segments" fallback
  and sets both ends to the same coordinate. X-positions comes out
  (1.2392 . 1.2392) and nothing can match it.

  The port's Beam::calc_x_positions is upstream's, line for line, INCLUDING
  that fallback -- upstream fed the same segments would answer the same thing.
  The divergence is upstream of all of it: the columns have not been placed, so
  the stem coordinates are not yet meaningful. That is line breaking and page
  layout.

  This is the FOURTH instance of one recorded pattern and it is worth naming as
  such: beams (draw with correct count but near-zero length), slurs (the
  slur search range explodes when the horizontal span collapses -- "upstream may
  do that unguarded because spacing has ALWAYS run by then"), repeats
  (Bracket::make_bracket divides by the bracket length), and now this. The
  earlier three were in C++ and could be guarded at upstream's own threshold.
  This one CANNOT be: the arithmetic is in scm/output-lib.scm, which is vendored
  byte-identical, and editing vendored upstream Scheme to survive a condition
  upstream never meets would be a parity bug wearing a fix's clothes.

  Why the file regressed at the scripts group rather than earlier: it fixed
  value_type_check refusing a procedure for a grob property, which is what had
  been silently throwing away \override Beam.stencil = #beam::slashed-stencil.
  Verified directly -- the same music with the stencil override removed still
  produces a page. Before then the default beam stencil drew and the file
  passed; the fix made the override real, and the code it now reaches needs
  spacing to have run.


  THREE MORE DEFECTS THE \layout FIX UNCOVERED

  Turning toplevel \layout blocks on ran machinery that had never executed, and the
  ratchet caught the consequences twice. All three were pre-existing; none is a cost
  of the fix.

  (1) equal? NEVER REACHED A HOST OBJECT'S EQUALITY HANDLER (LilyScheme).

  scm_equal_p ends by dispatching to a smob's equal_p; LilyScheme's SchemeEqual handled
  pairs, strings and vectors and then answered #f. LilyPond's moment<=? is
  (or (equal? a b) (ly:moment<? a b)), so "is this moment at or before that one"
  answered NO at the one moment they are the SAME. Measured directly: measurePosition
  printed as 0 while (moment<=? measurePosition ZERO-MOMENT) was #f.

  Measure_spanner_engraver therefore never cleared its finished spanner at
  stop-translation-timestep and fell through to its finalize branch -- which is
  scm/scheme-engravers.scm:306, (set! (ly:spanner-bound finished RIGHT) ...), an
  UPSTREAM bug: ly:spanner-bound is a plain LY_DEFINE with no setter half. Upstream
  never reaches it. This is the scripts group's
  assq-compares-boxed-numbers-by-reference finding
  one level up.

  Fixed with ISchemeEqual, the managed analogue of declaring an equal_p, on the model
  of IApplicable. The roster was READ OUT of the pinned source rather than guessed:
  exactly eight types declare a handler -- Duration, Moment, Listener, Input, Pitch,
  Tuplet_description, Prob and Spring. Spring's is literally scm_is_eq, so identity is
  already correct for it. Six are implemented, each delegating to an Equals override
  that was verified field-for-field against upstream's equal_p. PROB IS NOT: its
  handler is a real algorithm (it skips `origin' fields, compares class names, and
  walks both property alists), nothing measured demands it, and a half-right comparison
  would be worse than a recorded absence. OWED.

  The hook is opt-in on BOTH sides, matching Guile: a smob with no handler falls back
  to eq?, so implementing ISchemeEqual on a type whose upstream counterpart has none
  would answer #t where upstream answers #f.

  (2) A STALE UNIMPLEMENTED-THROW IN Measure_spanner::print.

  The bracket half threw, loudly and by name, saying it needed
  Bracket::make_axis_constrained_bracket "owed by the scripts group". The
  repeats group landed lily/bracket.cc
  WHOLE -- that function included -- while pulling it forward for its own two bracket
  grobs, and the throw was never taken back down. Nothing demanded it in between
  because the \layout block that turns Measure_spanner_engraver on was being discarded.
  Wired to the existing Bracket.MakeAxisConstrainedBracket; measure-spanner.cc's ledger
  row now reads WHOLE. Worth generalising: a named absence stops being true the moment
  its owner lands, and nothing re-checks it.

  (3) \language LEAKS ACROSS FILES IN THE BATCH RUNNER -- THE THIRD PER-FILE LEAK.

  ly:parser-set-note-names rebinds (lily)'s `pitchnames', which is SESSION state, and
  RestoreDefaults did not put it back. One regression file, midi-key-signature.ly,
  includes arabic.ly, whose first line is \language "italiano" -- so every file swept
  after it parsed with ITALIAN note names.

  This one had been costing real rows since well before the \layout work, and the
  sweep logs prove it rather than the directory: at the scripts group
  part-combine.ly and
  part-combine-relative.ly were already NOOUT on "not a note name: g", while
  part-combine-a2.ly and part-combine-inside-scale-durations.ly still PRODUCED PAGES --
  with 12 and 26 parse errors respectively. A file whose notes fail to parse still
  yields a page, just an emptier one, which is exactly how a leak this large stayed
  invisible. All four now produce pages with ZERO parse errors.

  It is the same shape as the other two per-file leaks and belongs beside them:
  \paper mutates in place, set-global-staff-size rebinds $defaultpaper, a toplevel
  \layout rebinds $defaultlayout, and \language rebinds pitchnames. Upstream needs
  none of it -- one file per process. Fenced by BatchRunnerTests.

  THE SINGLE-FILE-VS-SWEEP TRAP IS STILL LIVE, and it cost a bisect here:
  part-combine-a2.ly PASSES
  WHEN RUN ALONE and fails only inside the sweep. Bisecting the ordinal file list
  against the single victim found the culprit in nine runs; that is the technique to
  reach for, rather than a one-file probe, whenever a failure will not reproduce alone.


  (4) Context::check_removal WAS NEVER CALLED -- the deepest of the four.

  The port's Context.CheckRemoval is a faithful translation of upstream's
  check_removal, and NOTHING INVOKED IT. Upstream drives it from
  Global_context::run_iterator_on_me twice -- at the end of every timestep, right after
  apply_finalizations, and again after the iterator quits -- and both calls were absent
  from the port's loop.

  check_removal is what SENDS RemoveContext. So the port had the event defined, its
  listener registration wired, and the event never once fired. This is the
  lyrics group's
  Context_handle finding's sibling: same subsystem, same shape -- something declared
  upstream, half-reproduced, and silent because the one caller that depends on it had
  not been reached yet.

  The caller that finally reached it: recording-group-emulate in scm/part-combiner.scm
  registers a listener on RemoveContext whose whole job, per its own docstring, is to
  "add a final entry to record the end moment". \autoChange reads that entry as the
  final rest-mom of make-autochange-music's recursion; without it the recursion ends
  with rest-mom still #f and calls (skip-of-moment-span prev-change-mom #f), which dies
  in ly:moment-sub.

  MEASURED AGAINST THE ORACLE, not reasoned about -- and that mattered, because reading
  scm/autochange.scm alone suggests upstream should hit the same #f. Instrumenting
  recording-group-emulate under the pinned 2.27.2 binary for the same music reports TEN
  recorded entries ending at moment 2; the port reported NINE. The missing one is the
  end moment. ContextRemovalEndToEndTests fences the count at the oracle's ten, which is
  why it is a real fence and not a characterization of the port's own output.

  Fixed by adding both calls where upstream has them. auto-change, autochange-clefs and
  part-combine all produce pages with zero parse errors afterwards.


  WHAT THIS SESSION LEFT OPEN -- TWO ROWS, BOTH OWNED BY LATER GROUPS

  slashed-beam   Unplaced columns (line breaking / page layout); full account
                 above. Cannot be guarded,
                 because the arithmetic is in vendored upstream Scheme.
  nullvoice-change  Page layout's. Its toplevel expression is a \markup
                 \score-lines block, not
                 a score, and D20's score-level short-circuit skips toplevel non-score
                 entries -- the runner says so by name ("skipped toplevel non-score
                 entry"). It produced a page under the scripts group only
                 because the \language leak
                 kept the markup from parsing; parsing it correctly is what exposes the
                 known limit. Closes when the runner moves onto
                 ly:book-process.


================================================================================
MIDI: PERFORMERS, THE AUDIO LAYER, AND THE SMF WRITER
================================================================================

All 30 upstream files ported, with NO pull-forward of any other group's FILES. One
LEAF came forward from music-scheme.cc, ly:transpose-key-alist, and that file keeps
its long-tail disposition -- the same distinction stencil-scheme.cc has
carried since the scripts group, and the reason LedgerTests counts FILES while
EntryPointClosureTests counts
BINDINGS.

Scope is D27's: MIDI GENERATION plus the comparator and the oracle's references.
MIDI PLAYBACK is out of scope for LilyPort entirely and nothing here plays anything.


THE ONE STRUCTURAL DIVERGENCE, AND IT IS NOT OPTIONAL: byte[] REPLACES std::string

  Upstream's Midi_item::to_string and every to_string below it answer a std::string
  that is A BYTE CONTAINER. A 0x90 status byte and a UTF-8 lyric live in the same
  type and are concatenated without conversion, and the result is written to a file
  descriptor unchanged.

  System.String cannot do that. It is UTF-16, so a MIDI payload assembled as a string
  must choose an encoding on the way out, and no single choice is right for both a
  status byte in 0x80-0xFF (which wants Latin-1) and a lyric (which wants UTF-8).
  The whole MIDI layer is therefore byte[] end to end: MidiItem.ToBytes,
  MidiChunk.ToBytes, MidiEvent.ToBytes, MidiStream.Write.

  THIS IS THE ONE PLACE IN THE MIDI GROUP WHERE FOLLOWING THE LETTER OF
  UPSTREAM WOULD HAVE
  PRODUCED WRONG FILES RATHER THAN MERELY DIFFERENT CODE. Midi_text::to_string writes
  a variable-length LENGTH PREFIX taken from text_string_.length (), which is a BYTE
  count because a std::string already holds encoded bytes. A .NET string's Length is
  UTF-16 units, so every non-ASCII lyric would have been given a short length and
  silently truncated -- a file that still parses. Epg19Tests fences it with "é": one
  char, two bytes, and the prefix must read two.

  Flower gained BigEndianBytesU16/24/32 and HexToBytes for this. The existing
  char-based BigEndianU16/24/32 are untouched and keep their tests.


A DEFECT IN ALREADY-PORTED CODE, AND IT IS THE ONE TO REMEMBER:
Rational.FromDouble WAS THE ALGORITHM UPSTREAM EXPLICITLY WARNS AGAINST

  flower/rational.cc's Rational (double) is deliberately LOSSY: frexp, then twenty
  bits of mantissa (FACT = 1 << 20), normalize, apply the exponent as a shift,
  normalize again. Upstream comments on it directly:

      do not blindly substitute by libg++ code, since that uses
      arbitrary-size integers.  The rationals would overflow too easily.

  The port's FromDouble built the EXACT dyadic rational with BigInteger -- precisely
  the substitution that comment forbids -- and it overflowed exactly as predicted. A
  tempo ramp in the MIDI subsuite produced a double whose exact numerator does not fit
  a ulong; the conversion THREW; the exception escaped mid-write; and ELEVEN MIDI
  FILES were left truncated after their header, which the comparator reported as a
  verdict class of its own (UNPARSEABLE, the same class the repeats group met
  from ly:wide-char->utf-8).

  Corrected to upstream's algorithm. ONE deliberate divergence remains: where the
  exponent shift would exceed 63 bits upstream shifts a uint64_t past its width, which
  is UNDEFINED BEHAVIOUR in C++; the port saturates to zero or the signed infinity --
  the mathematical limit, and a defined answer where upstream has none.

  BLAST RADIUS, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: the closing sweep is byte-identical to
  the opening one (1,856 SVG / 281 NOOUT / 9 ERROR) and the ratchet reports 0
  regressions and 0 improvements. Musical durations are dyadic and small, so they
  survive the lossy path exactly; that is why nothing on the layout side moved.


THE FIFTH PER-FILE LEAK, AND IT IS THE OLDEST AND WIDEST FOUND SO FAR

  Lily_parser::default_duration_ is what a note with NO written duration inherits.
  Upstream makes ONE PARSER PER FILE, so every file starts at a quarter note. The port
  runs one session across the whole sweep, and nothing put it back -- so file N's last
  written duration became file N+1's default. A file that writes no durations at all
  writes nothing to correct it, so the error persisted for as long as the run did.

  It now sits beside its four siblings in LilyPondInit.RestoreDefaults:
      \paper mutates in place
      set-global-staff-size rebinds $defaultpaper
      a toplevel \layout rebinds $defaultlayout
      \language rebinds pitchnames
      the parser remembers default_duration_ (and default_tremolo_type_)

  FOUND THROUGH MIDI, WHICH IS WHY IT SURVIVED TRACK P, THE OUTPUT PIPELINE,
  HORIZONTAL SPACING, THE TEXT INTERFACE AND EVERY
  SWEEP SINCE. The layout comparator grades GLYPH INVENTORIES, and a page whose notes
  are all half length holds the same glyphs in the same order -- it is graded
  GLYPHS-DIFFER either way, for reasons that have nothing to do with duration. The MIDI
  comparator grades TICKS, and a note-off at 192 where the oracle says 384 is not a
  near miss. Measured both ways, and it is the single-file-vs-sweep trap in
  reverse:
  crescendo-return-crescendo.ly is CORRECT when run alone and halved inside the sweep.

  Worth generalising: a comparator can only catch what it grades. The layout ratchet
  has been blind to every timing error in the suite since it was seeded.


DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES (MIDI)

  - Midi_walker::operator++ becomes Advance(); finalize() becomes FinalizeTrack(),
    for the reason Translator::finalize became FinalizeTranslation -- Finalize is the
    C# destructor.
  - Performer::announce<T> (Args&&...) constructs and announces in one call. C#
    generics cannot express "construct T from these arbitrary arguments", so
    Announce<T> takes an already-constructed element. Same order of operations.
  - Midi_stream buffers and writes atomically on Dispose instead of writing to a
    file descriptor and renaming in a destructor. The property that matters -- a
    reader never sees a half-written file -- is kept. The ten-try O_EXCL loop has no
    analogue and is not reproduced.
  - Staff_performer's staff_map_ is a std::unordered_map upstream, whose iteration
    order is unspecified; get_audio_staff's `staff_map_.begin ()->second' therefore
    picks an ARBITRARY staff when more than one is present. The port uses insertion
    order, which is at least reproducible.
  - note-performer.cc and drum-note-performer.cc share their articulation walk
    VERBATIM upstream (it is copied and pasted between the two files). The port
    factors it into PerformerArticulations. This is the one duplication the
    MIDI group does not
    reproduce, and it is safe precisely because the two copies are identical line for
    line, so a single implementation cannot drift from either.
  - AudioTextType is a top-level enum, because upstream nests it as `Type' and that
    name is on standing rule 6's banned list. ITS VALUES ARE UPSTREAM'S and must stay
    so: they are written into the file as the meta-event type byte.
  - PerformerGroupPlaceholder, the empty stand-in TranslatorCreator returned for both
    Performer_group and Score_performer since the contexts group, IS RETIRED.

UPSTREAM CODE NOT CARRIED, AND WHY

  - Audio_element::gc_mark and Performance::derived_mark have no analogue: upstream
    marks each element's causing Stream_event so Guile's collector does not reclaim it,
    and in this port the reference IS the reachability.
  - Staff_performer::tempo_ IS DEAD UPSTREAM. It is declared and the only statement
    anywhere that touches it is `tempo_ = nullptr;' in stop_translation_timestep --
    nothing reads it and nothing assigns it a value. Reproducing it dead costs a
    CS0414 warning for a field that cannot affect anything.
  - Midi_control_change_announcer::announce_from_context_properties is DECLARED in the
    upstream header and DEFINED NOWHERE in pinned 2.27.2, with no caller.

A SILENT DEFECT THE PORT ALMOST SHIPPED, AND THE GENERAL LESSON

  Upstream reads numeric context properties with from_scm<Rational>. The obvious C#
  spelling is `GetProperty (...) is Rational', and it looks like it says the same
  thing. It does not: an exact Scheme ratio such as performer-init.ly's
  `tempoWholesPerMinute = #60/4' arrives as a LilyScheme Ratio, and an integer as a
  long, so the test misses both and the fallback wins. The symptom was a tempo span
  opening at MINUS INFINITY and every tempo in the subsuite computed over an infinite
  interval.

  SchemeConvert.TryToRational was added for this and every numeric property read in
  the MIDI group goes through it. Prefer it over a type test anywhere
  upstream writes
  from_scm<Rational>. Note that the pre-existing SchemeConvert.IsNumber does NOT count
  a flower Rational and is therefore not a substitute.

STATE THAT CAN LEAK, AND THE GUARD ADDED FOR IT

  Staff_performer keeps its instrument-to-channel map in STATIC members. Upstream's
  own mechanism -- the last Staff_performer to finalize clears them -- is reproduced
  faithfully, and StaffPerformer.ResetStaticChannelState was added for
  LilyPondInit.RestoreDefaults to call, because a score that dies before finalize would
  otherwise hand its channel assignments to the next file in the sweep. Upstream's own
  comment says these members "would prefer to be members of the containing class
  Performance"; this is that wish, minus the restructuring.

WHERE MIDI OUTPUT COMES FROM, AND WHAT STILL WAITS ON PAGE LAYOUT

  Upstream writes MIDI from Paper_book::output, which calls scm/midi.scm's
  write-performances-midis -- and that is reached through ly:book-process, which the
  port does not take (D20's score-level short-circuit). LilyPortPerformer is therefore
  the MIDI twin of LilyPortEngraver and BatchRunner drives both, per score, writing
  <base>.midi and <base>-<n>.midi exactly as write-performances-midis names them so the
  comparator can pair candidate to reference by name alone. When page layout
  moves the runner
  onto the real book path, both collapse into it.

  The MIDI half is a SEPARATE try/catch from the engraving half, deliberately:
  upstream produces a page and a .midi from the same score independently, so a score
  whose layout fails must still be able to perform. Folding them together would make
  every MIDI reference hostage to a layout gap that has nothing to do with it.

WHAT THE COMPARATOR SAYS TODAY, MEASURED AND NOT ROUNDED UP

  After a carry-forward pass closed the MIDI group's five families: 79 of the
  90 references MATCH the oracle event for event, 1 differs (a satb template whose
  layout side also fails, a finding from the line-breaking era), and 10 are
  missing -- every one an
  explicit \book file that D20's score-level short-circuit skips, so the whole missing
  set is page layout's. The original MIDI-group reading, kept for the record:
  68
  produced, 41 MATCH, 27 differ, 22 missing.

THE SEVENTH AND EIGHTH PER-FILE LEAKS

  (7) A TOPLEVEL \midi BLOCK REBINDS $defaultmidi -- the same shape as $defaultpaper
  (the text-interface find) and $defaultlayout (the ratchet-fix find).
  midi2ly-generated regression files set
  midiChannelMapping = #'instrument in exactly such a block, so every file swept after
  the first of them performed under 'instrument mapping instead of performer-init.ly's
  'staff -- visible as every staff landing on MIDI channel 0. Run alone the same file
  is correct: the single-file-vs-sweep trap, MIDI edition. LilyPondInit now
  snapshots and restores
  $defaultmidi beside its two siblings.

  (8) define-session VARIABLES ARE A LEAK CLASS, NOT A VARIABLE. Upstream re-initializes
  every `define-session' variable per file -- scm/lily.scm's session machinery exists
  for multi-file invocations -- and the port does not yet drive that machinery; it
  belongs to page layout with the real book path. The one variable MEASURED
  to leak,
  `unique-counter' (names \addlyrics' generated voices uniqueContext0, 1, ...), is put
  back to -1 by RestoreDefaults until then. Any other define-session variable is a
  candidate for the same failure shape.


================================================================================
CHORDS, FRETS, TAB, DRUMS, CLUSTERS, ARPEGGIO, FIGURED BASS
================================================================================

All 14 upstream files ported, with NO pull-forward of any other group's files and no
binding forced forward either. The group's ledger bucket is EMPTY. The entry-point
closure moves by FOURTEEN against fourteen files, which is the expected shape and not
a shortfall: eleven of the fourteen files are TRANSLATORS, and a translator has no
Scheme surface at all -- ly/engraver-init.ly names it in a \consists list and
TranslatorRegistry answers, so a binding is not how it is found.

D24 put the figured-bass trio here rather than with scripts/dynamics, and the
note it carried was followed: the landed side-position, column and alignment
code
was read first, because fingering columns and text-attached grobs are the trio's
closest technical cousins.

DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES

  Figure_group is a CLASS, not a struct (figured-bass-engraver.cc)
                 Upstream's is a value type held in a std::vector, and every use in
                 the file mutates a group IN PLACE through a reference:
                 `Figure_group &group = groups_[i];`. A C# struct in a List<T> hands
                 back a COPY, so every one of those mutations -- the continuation
                 line, the figure item, the six figure fields -- would be written to a
                 temporary and silently dropped. This is the one translation error
                 this file makes easy, and it would not have failed loudly: the page
                 would simply have had no extenders.

  derived_mark() is not carried (figured-bass-engraver.cc, fretboard-engraver.cc)
                 Both exist only to keep SCM members alive across a Guile garbage
                 collection. Managed fields need no such help, and the methods have no
                 other effect.

  the arrow glyph name uses the invariant culture (arpeggio.cc)
                 Upstream builds "scripts.arpeggio.arrow." + std::to_string (dir).
                 A culture whose negative sign is not U+002D would build a name the
                 font does not contain, and a missing glyph is SILENT here -- the
                 arpeggio would simply lose its arrowhead.

  the string/fret/finger list is walked ONCE (tab-note-heads-engraver.cc)
                 Upstream calls scm_list_ref inside the loop, which is quadratic in
                 the number of strings. The port walks with a cursor. Visiting order
                 and values are identical; only the cost differs, and the index i is
                 still tracked because `index` is computed from it.

  a zero-height squiggle cannot spin the arpeggio loop (arpeggio.cc)
                 Arpeggio::print stacks squiggles until they cover the chord, and
                 terminates because each adds its own height. A squiggle of ZERO
                 height would never terminate, and the symptom would be a sweep that
                 stops making progress with nothing in the log -- exactly the shape
                 the slur search range had. Upstream is protected by the music
                 font always carrying scripts.arpeggio; the port asks rather than
                 assumes, and reports. When the glyph is real the guard cannot fire
                 and the expression underneath it is upstream's, unchanged. This is
                 NOT one of the guards line breaking is meant to remove: it is
                 about a missing
                 glyph, not about spacing not having run.

  Figured_bass_position_engraver's assert becomes a programming error
                 Upstream asserts (!positioner_) on entry to start_spanner. The port
                 reports and returns rather than aborting the process, which is the
                 posture every other ported translator takes.

WHAT UPSTREAM DECLARES AND NEVER DEFINES

  Figured_bass_continuation::print is DECLARED in figured-bass-continuation.cc and
  has NO definition anywhere in the pinned tree. It is therefore NOT an entry point
  and no binding is made for it: define-grobs.scm names `figured-bass-continuation::
  print', with no ly: prefix, which scm/output-lib.scm defines. That file contributes
  exactly ONE binding, ly:figured-bass-continuation::center-on-figures. Same shape as
  the Slur::vertical_skylines declaration the slurs group recorded -- and
  worth generalising: a
  DECLARE_SCHEME_CALLBACK is not evidence that an entry point exists.

A DEFECT IN ALREADY-PORTED CODE, FOUND BY PORTING A CALLER

  ly_assoc had never been carried, and SchemeUtilities.LyAssocGet carried a RECORDED
  NARROWING in its place: it went straight to Assq, so a key that is neither a symbol
  nor an immediate was compared by IDENTITY where upstream compares by equal?. The
  note said "no caller in the engine uses such a key today". Drum_notes_engraver is
  the first that does -- it looks a percussion sign up in scriptDefinitions through
  ly_assoc -- so ly_assoc is now implemented from lily-guile.hh's own branch
  (symbol or SCM_IMP -> assq, otherwise assoc) and LyAssocGet goes through it. The
  narrowing is closed. Fenced from both sides by Epg20Tests: a string key must be
  found, and an absent key must still answer the fallback.

  Generalise it the way the ratchet fixes generalised their stale throw: a
  recorded narrowing
  stops being harmless the moment a caller arrives, and nothing re-checks it.

FIRST CALLER OF ARTICULATIONS.CC

  articulation_list has been ported since Wave A with NO caller anywhere in the
  engine. Fretboard_engraver and Tab_note_heads_engraver are its first two. It was
  read against upstream before being relied on and is faithful, including the
  awkwardness it exists for: a string number written INSIDE a chord arrives as an
  articulation on the note event, one written OUTSIDE arrives as an event of its own,
  and the two have to come out as one positionally-aligned list.

AN UPSTREAM QUIRK REPRODUCED RATHER THAN TIDIED

  Figure_group::assign_from_event takes `currevt' and reads the FIGURE off
  `current_event_' instead -- every other field comes from currevt. The one call site
  passes the same event for both, so the two are indistinguishable in practice.
  Reproduced as written: tidying it would be a guess about which upstream meant.

WHAT THE SWEEP SAYS

  Arpeggio_engraver, Cluster_spanner_engraver and Note_head_line_engraver were tied
  at the top of the unported-translator demand list at 4,132 misses per sweep each,
  with Figured_bass_engraver at 3,429 and ly:chord-bracket::print at 96. All are now
  registered and reached.

================================================================================
LINE BREAKING & BROKEN SPANNERS
================================================================================

All 12 upstream files ported, with NO pull-forward of any other group's FILES. The
group's ledger bucket is EMPTY. Three LEAVES of grob-scheme.cc came forward, forced
by the demand loop rather than chosen; that file keeps its long-tail
disposition, the same distinction stencil-scheme.cc has carried since the
scripts group.

THE GROUP IS MUCH BIGGER THAN ITS FILE LIST, and the reason is worth generalising:
FIVE FILES WHOSE LEDGER ROWS HAVE SAID `ported' FROM THE START WERE HOLLOW ON
THEIR
BREAK-PROCESSING HALVES -- grob.cc, spanner.cc, item.cc, system.cc and
axis-group-interface.cc, whose ENTIRE pure-height half was absent. Nothing asked for
any of it until line breaking did. A `ported' row means a file was visited, not that
it is whole.

DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES

  One score's lines are stacked with a fixed padding (LilyPortEngraver)
                 Upstream assembles lines onto pages in Page_layout_problem, which is
                 page layout's, reading system-system-spacing and the rest.
                 Until the runner
                 moves onto the real ly:book-process path, a score's lines are stacked
                 top to bottom at the SAME fixed padding BatchRunner already uses
                 between two SCORES. Drawing every line is not optional -- it is the
                 whole visible effect of the group -- so the choice is between this
                 stand-in and drawing one line in four. Retires with D20's book path.

  fast_substitute_grob_array declines the fast path on an empty range
                 (BreakSubstitution) Upstream forbids the case with an assert. The
                 port cannot: an empty Slice made it subtract one sentinel from
                 another, overflow into a small positive range and index off the end
                 of its table. It falls back to the slow path, which gives the same
                 answer.

WHAT THE CLOSE-OUT DISCHARGED

  The whole INHERIT LIST that earlier groups parked on this group is gone, and every
  guard was REMOVED and RE-MEASURED rather than kept:

    * The two slur search-range guards (SlurScoring). The expression under them
      is upstream's, and it divides by the distance between the slur's two base
      attachments. It was safe to restore because spacing now runs before any stencil
      is asked for. Measured: no stall, and tuplet-slurs and tuplet-slur-tweaks came
      back from MISSING.
    * The zero-length guard in Bracket::make_bracket. Restored to upstream's
      unconditional division.
    * The null answer in Spanner::find_broken_piece and its hara-kiri omission
      in
      System::get_neighboring_staff. Both restored to upstream; consider_suicide runs
      on each candidate because hara-kiri-group-spanner.cc is ported now.
    * The null-bound guard in Beam::common_with_bound. Restored to upstream's
      unconditional dereference: Spanner::do_break_processing is what guarantees the
      bound, and it exists now. An unbounded beam reaching print again would fail
      loudly on that line, which is the honest outcome.
    * The pure-height stand-ins. Grob::pure_y_extent reads through the real
      call_pure_function, and Align_interface::get_skylines takes upstream's pure path.

DEFECTS FOUND IN ALREADY-PORTED CODE

  Align_interface::get_skylines asked for STENCILS in its PURE branch
                 It called g->extent (g, Y_AXIS) where upstream calls pure_y_extent,
                 so SPACING pulled real stencils out of grobs whose spanners had not
                 been broken yet. That is what drew a VoltaBracket with both bounds on
                 unplaced columns and put a NaN through the skyline builder
                 (volta-multi-staff-inner-staff.ly, whose oracle run reports no such
                 error). The pure path exists so that this question can be answered
                 WITHOUT a stencil; answering it with one is not an approximation but
                 a different computation, and it was also four times slower.

  Grob::internal_get_pure_property had never come across (grob-property.cc)
                 Another hollow half, found when the real pure path demanded
                 ly:grob-pure-property. Nothing had asked while every pure read was
                 answered by an ordinary extent.

  Paper_column_engraver was missing three more halves besides handle_manual_breaks
                 No listen_break and no handle_manual_breaks, so \break, \noBreak,
                 \pageBreak and \pageTurn had NO effect of their own -- invisible while
                 every column was breakable by accident, load-bearing the moment the
                 carry-forward pass restored the permission-stripping block.
                 No
                 listen_label, so page labels never reached a column. And no
                 process_music AT ALL, so nothing ever wrote `measure-length' onto a
                 command column -- a property Spacing_spanner and Multi_measure_rest
                 both READ, and both had been reading an unset value since
                 horizontal spacing landed.

  Hara_kiri_group_spanner::calc_skylines was wrong in both halves
                 It called combine_skylines, which upstream reserves for an axis group
                 whose children are all axis groups, where upstream calls
                 skyline_spacing; and it answered the SkylinePair OBJECT where the
                 property holds a Scheme cons of two skylines. There is no
                 skyline-pair type in Scheme, so every answer failed its own property's
                 type check and vertical-skylines was left UNSET on every hara-kiri
                 group in every score.

  Only the first line of every score was ever drawn (LilyPortEngraver)
                 Engrave() took paperSystems[0] and discarded the rest. Invisible while
                 PlaceColumnsOnOneLine made every score one line; the moment line
                 breaking landed it became the group's entire visible effect, because
                 the port CHOSE three lines for break.ly and drew one.

A MEASUREMENT THAT WAS WRONG, AND IS NOW CORRECTED

  The sweep log's "N system(s)" column NEVER counted systems. It counted the number
  of stencils the runner stacked, which is one per SCORE -- accidental-styles.ly
  reported 20 systems before line breaking existed at all, because it holds 20 scores.
  It now reports the sum of the LINES each score broke into. Any comparison of that
  column against a sweep log from before the multi-page naming change is
  comparing two different
  quantities; the SVG/NOOUT/ERROR verdicts either side of that date are unaffected.

================================================================================
PAGE BREAKING AND PAGE LAYOUT
================================================================================

Files ported: page-breaking.cc(+scheme), page-layout-problem.cc(+scheme),
page-spacing.cc, page-spacing-result.cc, optimal/minimal/one-page/
one-line-page/one-line-auto-height/page-turn-page-breaking.cc,
page-turn-engraver.cc, footnote-engraver.cc, paper-book.cc(+scheme), and
book-scheme.cc's ly:book-process / ly:book-process-to-systems. Seventeen ledger
rows; the group's bucket is EMPTY.

D20 IS DISCHARGED. The batch runner no longer short-circuits at score level.
It runs Book::process, which builds a Paper_book, scales and normalizes its
paper, renders every score through Score::book_rendering, and lets the paper
block's own `page-breaking' procedure choose the pages. The runner then writes
ONE FILE PER PAGE with framework-svg.scm's own naming -- <base>.svg for a
single-page book and <base>-1.svg upwards for a multi-page one. The previous
divergence note (§ "THE BATCH RUNNER runs D20's short-circuit") is retired by
this entry.

DELIBERATE DIVERGENCES (page breaking)

  paper-book.cc -- THE OUTPUT HALF IS NOT PORTED. Paper_book::output,
    classic_output, output_stencil, output_stencils and dump_header_fields
    dispatch into `lily framework-<backend>' modules and act on the
    -dclip-systems / -dpreview / -dcrop / -daux-files program options. That is
    the CLI surface D14 replaced with Lily.Shell and the PS/Cairo backends D19
    replaced. PAGE ASSEMBLY -- Pages(), which is what every caller in this port
    actually needs -- is ported in full.

  ly:book-process / ly:book-process-to-systems ANSWER THE PAPER BOOK where
    upstream answers SCM_UNSPECIFIED. Upstream can discard it because output()
    has already written the files; here the caller (the batch runner, Lily.Shell)
    needs the pages back. Scheme callers that ignore the result -- every one in
    ly/init.ly -- are unaffected. Both bindings FORCE the pages before
    returning, because a lazily-empty paper book is indistinguishable from a
    book with nothing in it.

  page-turn-page-breaking.cc -- print_break_node is not ported. It exists only
    behind -ddebug-page-breaking-scoring, which this port does not carry.

  page-layout-problem.cc -- the spacing-spec readers (is_spaceable,
    read_spacing_spec, get_spacing_spec, get_fixed_spacing, add_stretchability)
    stay in Layout/PageLayoutSpacing.cs, where vertical organization pulled
    them forward because
    Align_interface reads every adjacent pair of staves through them. They are
    NOT duplicated here.

DEFECTS FOUND IN ALREADY-PORTED CODE (page breaking)

  ly:format-output READ THE PROPERTY OFF THE WRONG CONTEXT. Upstream's
    Global_context::get_output does `get_property (get_score_context (),
    "output")', and Score_engraver::finalize sets it on its OWN context -- the
    Score, a CHILD of Global. The port read it off Global, and context lookup
    walks UPWARD, so it never found it: ly:format-output and
    ly:score-embedded-format both answered nothing. Invisible because the only
    path the sweep exercised, LilyPortEngraver, reaches the paper score by
    walking the tree for the ScoreEngraver instead of by asking for it.

  THE SVG DOCUMENT WAS NEVER SCALED BY output-scale. framework-svg.scm's
    output-stencil computes `svg-width = output-scale * device-width' for the
    width and height attributes and passes the UNSCALED extents to the view box.
    The port omitted the factor, so every page it has ever written declared
    1/output-scale of its true size -- an A4 page came out 119.5 x 169.0mm
    instead of 210 x 297. Fixed in SvgBackend.RenderDocument.

  output-def-scheme.cc WAS MISSING ly:paper-get-number while its ledger row read
    `ported'. lily/page.scm asks for it on the very first page it builds. The
    file's disposition is unchanged -- the stencil-scheme.cc pattern.

  score.cc AND book.cc WERE HOLLOW ON THEIR RENDERING HALVES. Both rows read
    `ported'; Score::book_rendering and Book::process/process_score/
    process_bookparts/error_found/add_scores_to_bookpart were simply absent.
    Same shape line breaking found in five files at once.

  ConstrainedBreaking.GetLineDetails HANDS BACK ITS LIVE DP-TABLE OBJECTS.
    Upstream only survives mutating them because Line_details is a STRUCT copied
    on every assignment. Ported literally, Page_breaking::calc_line_heights
    writes each page configuration's tallness straight back into the line
    breaker's own table and leaks it into the next configuration. LineDetails
    now carries Copy(), and PageBreaking copies at both points upstream copies.

TRAPS WORTH NOT REDISCOVERING (page breaking)

  THE UNSIGNED-VPOS TRAP IS REAL AND IT COST THIS SESSION A SWEEP.
    Page_spacer::calc_subproblem guards its cell update with
    `if (page > 0 || page_start == 0)'. When the page count is UNCONSTRAINED
    upstream passes page = VPOS, which is the LARGEST unsigned value, so
    `page > 0' is TRUE. Ported literally against NoPosition (-1) the test
    INVERTS: the only cell ever written is page_start == 0, so every line's best
    solution becomes "put lines 0..line on ONE page" and the unconstrained
    solver answers a single page for any book of any height. It is not a subtle
    mis-scoring -- it is the entire page-breaking decision, and it fails
    silently with a plausible-looking page. Every `> 0' and `< size' test
    against a vsize that may hold VPOS needs reading twice.


-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ADDED AT THE PAGE-BREAKING CLOSE-OUT -- FIVE BINDINGS THAT WERE NEVER
REGISTERED
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every defect this session found was in code whose LEDGER ROW ALREADY SAID
`ported', and every one of them was silent. The shape repeats often enough now
to state as a rule:

  A PORTED TYPE WITH AN UNREGISTERED BINDING IS WORSE THAN AN UNPORTED TYPE.
  The C# side works, every C# caller works, the ledger row is honest about the
  FILE, and the Scheme side quietly receives an UnportedValue placeholder --
  which is inert, so it flows politely through whatever asked for it. Nothing
  fails. PORT-COVERAGE's own early note predicted this in as many words
  ("one Scheme call away from being actively wrong"); it took until page
  breaking to
  come true, twice.

  ly:context-def-modify, ly:context-def-lookup  (context-def.cc)

    NEITHER WAS REGISTERED. Context_def itself was ported whole -- Clone,
    AddContextMod, Lookup and IsAlias all existed -- so both bindings are
    one-line calls onto methods already there.

    The cost was not a missing feature. lily-library.scm's
    `context-defs-from-music', which EVERY toplevel \layout block containing a
    context modification runs through, does

        (ly:output-def-set-variable! output-def (car entry)
                                     (ly:context-def-modify (cdr entry) mods))

    so each context definition the block touched was OVERWRITTEN with the
    placeholder. Measured: `\layout { \override StaffSymbol.staff-space = 1.23 }'
    took the layout from 43 context definitions to 20 -- every one of the 23
    that answer to `Bottom' destroyed -- and `\layout { \markLengthOn }'
    destroyed all four that answer to `Score'. The score then had no context to
    engrave into and the file produced NO PAGES AT ALL. The only diagnostic was
    "cannot create default child context: Voice", which names neither the
    \layout block, nor the binding, nor the context definitions.

    Seven regression files were dying this way. A plain `\layout { ragged-right
    = ##t }' was unaffected, which is what made the defect look absent: a
    variable assignment never reaches context-defs-from-music at all.

  ly:system::get-staves, ly:system::get-spaceable-staves,
  ly:system::get-nonspaceable-staves  (system.cc)

    One upstream function under a filter, and its only caller is
    paper-system.scm's `paper-system-annotate', which takes (length ...) of the
    answer on the very next line. So `\paper { annotate-spacing = ##t }' -- two
    words -- took the whole book down with wrong-type-arg ("length" "Not a
    proper list"), naming neither the paper variable nor the callback.

    Registered here with page breaking rather than with system.cc's other
    callbacks
    because they are page-layout surface: annotate-spacing is what wants them.

THE DEPRECATED-PROPERTY PATH WAS ONLY HALF PORTED (lily-guile.cc)

  Deprecated_property::getter_desc was wired into ly:context-property. The
  SETTER half -- internal_type_check's deprecation branch and type_check_unset
  -- was not, and SchemeUtilities.TypeCheckAssignment said so in a comment:
  "until the deprecation path is ported the property is simply unknown".

  The cost was that `\unset Timing.<deprecated>' was DISCARDED. A file that set
  skipTypesetting, then unset it through the deprecated alias, never turned
  typesetting back on -- and engraved nothing at all, with no diagnostic.

  Two structural changes came with it, both required rather than incidental:

  * THE PREDICATE LOOKUP NOW COMES FIRST. The port ran value_type_check's
    short-circuits ('()/#f/*unspecified*, and backend-type?'s procedures) BEFORE
    asking whether the property exists, so an unknown name carrying any of those
    values was accepted in silence -- and the deprecation branch was unreachable
    for exactly the case that needs it most, since \unset passes no value at
    all. Upstream asks for the predicate first; only a property that HAS one
    gets the short-circuits.

  * TypeCheckAssignment ANSWERS A SYMBOL AND A VALUE, not just a bool. A
    deprecated property redirects to its replacement AND converts its value on
    the way (the description carries an old->new function precisely because a
    rename is not always only a rename). The bool overload is kept for
    backend-type? and music-type?, the two categories scm/lily.scm leaves
    unwired, and says in its own doc why it is safe there and nowhere else.

  DeprecatedProperty now threads the CATEGORY through
  deprecated-setter-object-property instead of hardcoding the translation
  table, which is upstream's indirection and exists so a backend or music
  deprecation table can be added without silently consulting the wrong one.

ly:grob-system USED THE WRONG FUNCTION ON THE WRONG AXIS (grob-scheme.cc)

  grob-scheme.cc:349 calls the VIRTUAL me->get_system(): Grob answers 0, Item
  answers from its X parent, Paper_column answers the system it was assigned,
  and Spanner (spanner.cc:336) answers from its TWO BOUNDS when both agree. The
  port instead walked Y PARENTS -- which is not that function, and is not
  upstream's static Grob::get_system(Grob *) helper either, since that walks X
  parents and is documented as the PRE-line-breaking version.

  All four levels of the virtual were already ported correctly. Only the
  binding was wrong, so a spanner whose bounds knew their system still answered
  nothing. Second half of the same divergence: upstream answers SCM_EOL, the
  port answered #f, and '() is TRUE in Scheme -- so `(and (ly:grob-system g)
  ...)' took different branches in the two.

  ⚠ FIXING THE TRUTHINESS ALONE FIXES NOTHING, and the previous session's note
  said so: bar-line.scm hands the answer to ly:grob-extent, which rejects '() as
  firmly as #f. The first half is the substantive one. Three ratchet rows came
  back with it.

THE KEEP-THE-PARSER-CURRENT WRAPPER DOES NOT RETIRE (BatchRunner)

  Page breaking inherited it as item 3 of three, on the premise that moving
  the runner
  onto the real ly:book-process path would make it unnecessary. THAT PREMISE IS
  WRONG AND WAS MEASURED WRONG: with the runner fully on the book
  path, removing the wrapper puts apply-output, fermata-dot-position,
  markup-rhythm-ragged and flags-straight-layout-staff-size straight back on
  "there is no current parser".

  The book path is not the parser's DYNAMIC EXTENT. Upstream reaches book
  processing from default-toplevel-book-handler, which the parser calls WHILE
  PARSING, so %parser is live by construction. This runner collects books
  during the parse and processes them after it, so it must publish %parser
  itself. AsCurrentParser is upstream's own fluid, not a workaround. Retiring
  it would mean processing each book from inside the toplevel handler, which is
  a design decision rather than a clean-up.

LilyPortEngraver's FIXED PADDING IS RETIRED (inherited item 2)

  The one-score API stacked a score's lines at a hardcoded 4.0. It now takes
  the offsets from the REAL Page_layout_problem, built with NO paper book and
  NO page -- upstream's own supported shape for a book-less problem, and the
  honest one here, because this API engraves one score against an UNSCALED
  layout and has no page to read system-system-spacing off. Anything wanting
  the paper's real spacing wants a book, and that path is BatchRunner.

  BatchRunner's own StackSystems was DEAD CODE by then and is deleted.

================================================================================
ANCIENT NOTATION
================================================================================

All twelve ledger rows landed in one session: the ligature engraver hierarchy
(Ligature_engraver, Coherent_ligature_engraver, Gregorian_ligature_engraver --
all three abstract), the four concrete ligature engravers, Episema_engraver,
and the four ligature grobs. Ledger 394 -> 406 ported, 25 -> 13 owed, and the
group's bucket is EMPTY. Entry points 673 -> 678.

THE GROUP CLOSED THE WHOLE UNPORTED-TRANSLATOR DEMAND LIST

  The close-out sweep asked for exactly five translator names it could not
  resolve, and all five are this group's:

    Ligature_bracket_engraver     4,224 misses
    Episema_engraver                 54
    Mensural_ligature_engraver       44
    Vaticana_ligature_engraver       19
    Kievan_ligature_engraver         18

  ⚠ READ THE 4,224 CORRECTLY. It is not a measure of how much ancient music the
  suite contains -- it is one per Voice context per score, because
  Ligature_bracket_engraver is in the DEFAULT Voice. Every score in the suite
  asked for it and every score engraved anyway, because an unresolved \consists
  is a warning and nothing more. After this group the list is empty but for
  Rhythmic_column_engraver_foo, which is a test input's deliberate typo.

ABSTRACT BASES: THE ACKNOWLEDGERS MOVED UP, THE SET DID NOT CHANGE

  Upstream declares ADD_ACKNOWLEDGER (rest) and ADD_ACKNOWLEDGER (ligature_head)
  in each of the three CONCRETE subclasses of Ligature_engraver, and one
  ADD_DELEGATE_LISTENER (ligature) alongside, because a C++ base class cannot run
  its subclass's boot (). The port registers all of them in LigatureEngraver
  itself. The set is identical: all three subclasses declare exactly those, and
  Ligature_bracket_engraver -- which declares note_column instead of
  ligature_head -- is NOT a subclass of Ligature_engraver, upstream or here.

  The `Grob_info_t<Item>' narrowing upstream's dispatcher applies BEFORE the hook
  runs is an `is Item' test here. It is not cosmetic: without it a non-item
  carrying ligature-head-interface would be collected as a null primitive and
  every later pass over the array would dereference it.

DEAD UPSTREAM, KEPT DEAD

  Two members of Ligature_engraver are written or declared and read or called by
  NOTHING in the whole upstream tree: ligature_start_mom_ (assigned in
  process_music) and current_ligature () (a defined virtual). Both are ported
  because they are part of the class's declared shape. ligature_start_mom_ is a
  property rather than a field only so that "assigned but never read" is not a
  build warning.

  Coherent_ligature_engraver::collect_accidentals has a bare `/* TODO */' body
  upstream and has one here. Making it do something would be a divergence, not
  an improvement -- accidentals inside a ligature stay where they are in both.

  mensural-ligature.cc's `#if 0 /* what happend with the ledger lines? */' block
  and coherent-ligature-engraver.cc's `if constexpr (false)' spacing-collapse
  block are dead by construction upstream and are not ported; the comments that
  name them are kept.

⚠ UPSTREAM'S OWN TYPO IS REPRODUCED, NOT CORRECTED

  gregorian-ligature-engraver.cc's fix_prefix_set ends with

    fix_prefix ("pes_or_flexa", LINEA, current_set, min_set, max_set, primitive);

  -- the name says pes_or_flexa and the mask is LINEA, so LINEA is checked twice
  and PES_OR_FLEXA never. The port reproduces it exactly. Fixing it would change
  which warnings a Gregorian score emits, and parity is the job here. Recorded so
  that a future reader does not "fix" it either.

⚠ Bezier IS A CLASS IN Flower, SO UPSTREAM'S COPY-ON-ASSIGN NEEDED WRITING OUT

  vaticana-ligature.cc's flexa maker is built on

    Bezier top_curve = curve, bottom_curve = curve;

  which COPIES in C++ and would ALIAS in C#, because Flower's Bezier is a sealed
  class rather than a struct. All three names would be the same object and every
  control-point adjustment would land on all three, silently, producing a
  degenerate shape rather than an error. VaticanaLigature.CopyOf makes the copy
  explicit and is commented as load-bearing at both ends.

  This is a defect CLASS, not one site: any port of upstream code that assigns a
  Bezier by value has it. No other current site does -- checked -- but the next
  one will.

from_scm<bool> IS EXACTLY-#t, AND THIS GROUP DEPENDS ON IT

  scm_conversions<bool>::from_scm defaults its fallback to false, which makes it
  scm_is_eq (s, SCM_BOOL_T) -- NOT Scheme truth. Gregorian_ligature_engraver
  MULTIPLIES the result by a bit mask, so a truthy-but-not-#t value would have to
  come out 1 anyway; but Mensural_ligature_engraver branches on ligature-flexa,
  ligature-pes, left-down-stem, right-down-stem and right-up-stem, and there an
  unset property read as true would change every shape in the ligature. The port
  uses SchemeUtilities.ToBool (exactly-#t) throughout, never IsSchemeTrue -- the
  defect class the carry-forward pass named.

PITCH READS FALL BACK TO 0 WHERE UPSTREAM DEREFERENCES A NULL

  calc_delta_pitches, provide_context_info, Mensural_ligature_engraver's
  transform_heads and Vaticana_ligature_engraver's ambiguous-dot check all read

    unsmob<Pitch> (get_property (cause, "pitch"))->steps ()

  with no null check. Upstream segfaults if a ligature primitive has no pitch;
  the port answers 0. The case is unreachable in valid input -- rests are refused
  by acknowledge_rest and transform_heads checks note-event before it reads the
  pitch -- so this is a crash-safety choice, not a behaviour change.

WHAT THE GROUP MEASURABLY CHANGED

  Measured by disabling Mensural_ligature_engraver's registration and re-running
  the same file, which is the only honest way to attribute output to a group:

    mensural-ligatures.ly    0 -> 174 polygons, and the oracle has 174 exactly.
                             2 pages -> 1 page, and the oracle writes 1.

  The ligature shapes ARE the polygons: without the engraver the heads are drawn
  as ordinary glyphs and no connected geometry exists at all. The page count
  follows from move_related_items_to_column folding each ligature's heads into
  ONE paper column, which is most of what a ligature does to spacing.

  mensural-ligature-citations.ly went MISSING -> GLYPHS-DIFFER: it produced no
  output before this group and produces its three pages now.

⚠ A PRE-EXISTING LEDGER-LINE OVER-DRAW IS VISIBLE HERE AND IS NOT THIS GROUP'S

  mensural-ligatures.ly draws a family of ledger-line rects (height 0.23 =
  staff-line thickness 0.13 + staff-space * 0.1) that the oracle does not draw at
  all. The same rects are present WITHOUT this group's engravers -- 464 of them,
  which this group REDUCES to 368 by folding heads into shared columns. It
  belongs to whoever owns ledger lines on ancient staves, and it is recorded here
  only because a reader comparing this file against the oracle will see it first.

WARNINGS: THE ORACLE'S LOGS CANNOT SETTLE THEM

  The port emits 11 "flexa cannot have stem in the middle" warnings on
  mensural-ligatures.ly and the reference log for that file is EMPTY. That is not
  a divergence: generate-reference.sh runs the oracle with --silent, so NOT ONE of
  the 2,316 reference logs contains a `warning:' line. Whether upstream warns has
  to be settled by reading upstream, and it does warn here -- pairwise flexa
  formation leaves every second note with a previous head whose shape is
  MLP_FLEXA_END, which fails prev_brevis_shape, which is exactly that branch. The
  port's warning columns match that trace note for note.

TESTS

  Epg21Tests (11, Engine.Tests) fences the two things this group can get wrong
  invisibly: the bit masks -- asserted as the RELATIONSHIPS upstream defines them
  by, not as literals, so a renumbering cannot satisfy both sides -- and the
  registrations. AncientNotationEndToEndTests (9, LilyPort.Tests) fences the
  shapes, each fact against a control that must come out differently.


================================================================================
PAGE BREAKING CLOSE-OUT: THE SPRING SEAM AND THE STAFF-LINE MEASUREMENT
================================================================================

This pass was opened to close page breaking's ten red ratchet rows. It found
and fixed one defect in page breaking's OWN machinery, restored one hollow
branch in an already-`ported' file, and MEASURED a suite-wide defect that is
not page breaking's and
that the ten rows all sit on. Nothing was re-baselined; the floor is untouched
at 1,815 / 501 and the gate still reads ten.

DEFECT 1 -- A MUTABLE STRUCT IN A LIST: THREE SILENT LOSSES IN append_system

  Spring is a STRUCT here and upstream mutates `springs_.back ()'. C#'s
  List<T> indexer answers a COPY for a value type, so

      _springs[_springs.Count - 1].EnsureMinDistance (x);

  compiles with zero warnings, mutates a temporary and throws it away. Three
  mutations in Page_layout_problem::append_system were being discarded:

    1. the "leave room for any loose lines above this system" floor;
    2. ensure_min_distance on EVERY spring BETWEEN two staves;
    3. the whole alignment-distances block -- set_ideal_distance,
       set_min_distance and set_inverse_stretch_strength -- so a score that
       explicitly placed its staves was IGNORED OUTRIGHT.

  ⚠ THE SITES WERE INCONSISTENT, WHICH IS THE SIGNATURE OF THIS TRAP. The
  constructor's last_spring and append_prob's spring_copy both mutate-then-add
  and were always correct; append_system's three add-then-mutate and were
  always wrong. Whoever ported the file was aware of the seam in some places
  and not in others, and nothing distinguishes the two shapes by eye.

  Fixed with a ref-returning LastSpring(), which is what upstream's
  `springs_.back ()' actually means. MEASURED: 47 pages changed geometry,
  ZERO verdicts changed, zero regressions.

  ⚠ THIS IS THE ANCIENT-NOTATION Bezier FINDING IN THE MIRROR, and the pair
  should be read
  as one rule: EVERY C++ VALUE/REFERENCE DECISION IS A PORT DECISION. There a
  C++ value was aliased by a C# reference; here a C++ reference was copied by a
  C# value. Both are silent and neither shows up in a diff against upstream.

  THE CLASS WAS SWEPT, not just the site. Skyline and SkylinePair are sealed
  CLASSES; beginLineHeights/midLineHeights, _dimensionCache and _pedalInfo are
  ARRAYS, whose element access is a genuine ref for value types; DrulArray's
  indexer answers by value but its only mutable-struct use is a read. The
  List<Spring> in PageLayoutProblem is the only instance in the engine.

DEFECT 2 -- Paper_column::break_align_width WAS HOLLOW ON ITS ELSE BRANCH

  Upstream walks the requested align symbols and, for anything that is not the
  literal `break-alignment', calls
  Break_alignment_interface::find_nonempty_break_align_group. That entire else
  branch was ABSENT: the port's loop matched only `break-alignment' and skipped
  every named group before it. A StaffSymbol asks with
  (staff-bar break-alignment), so `staff-bar' was never consulted.

  ⚠ THE FUNCTION IT SHOULD CALL WAS ALREADY PORTED -- line breaking landed
  find_nonempty_break_align_group in BreakAlignInterface.cs. This caller was
  written while it did not exist and NOTHING RE-CHECKED IT once it did. That is
  the ratchet fixes' own rule (a named absence stops being true the moment its
  owner lands) meeting page breaking's (a ported type with a missing call is
  worse than an
  unported type), in a file whose ledger row has read `ported' throughout.

  Restored faithfully. MEASURED HONESTLY: it changed NOTHING in the sweep --
  zero pages, zero verdicts -- because the BreakAlignment it would search has
  no non-empty groups to find in the first place (see the finding below). It is
  correct, it is inert today, and it is recorded as both.

THE FINDING THIS SESSION IS REALLY ABOUT -- COLLAPSED STAFF LINES, SUITE-WIDE

  Measured over the whole closing sweep, counting only HORIZONTAL <line>
  elements (a vertical bar line or stem has x1 == x2 and must not be counted --
  the first measurement of this made exactly that mistake and read 73.7%):

                    horizontal <line>   collapsed (<0.5)   pages affected
      oracle              32,254          1,568  ( 4.9%)         145
      port                22,385         16,664  (74.4%)       1,296

  THREE QUARTERS OF THE PORT'S STAFF LINES ARE DRAWN WITH NO LENGTH. They come
  out as x1="0.0500" x2="-0.0500" -- running BACKWARDS, the full width being the
  line thickness -- where the oracle draws, e.g., x1="0.0500" x2="80.7122".

  ⚠ THE COMPARATOR HAS NEVER REPORTED THIS AND STRUCTURALLY CANNOT. It grades
  glyph inventory and position; a staff line is a <line>, and a page whose
  staff lines are collapsed holds the same glyphs in the same order. This is
  the MIDI group's timing blindness in a second dimension, and the general
  rule it
  argues for is now stated twice: A GREEN RATCHET IS EVIDENCE ABOUT WHAT THE
  COMPARATOR GRADES AND ABOUT NOTHING ELSE.

  Traced two levels. Staff_symbol::print is a faithful translation and its
  span_points arrive as exactly (0,0) -- the initial value, untouched by both
  branches. The BREAKALIGN branch is the one that fires, and
  Paper_column::break_align_width answers a POINT interval because (a) the
  BreakAlignment's extent is empty and (b) the bound column's coordinate
  relative to its system is 0. Defect 2 above is on that path and does not
  change it.

  IT IS NOT LINE BREAKING ALONE, MEASURED: unbroken single-line scores are
  62.9% collapsed and broken ones 81.9%. Line breaking worsens it; it does not
  cause it.

  OWNER: NOT PAGE BREAKING. ly:break-alignment-interface::calc-positioning-done
  is
  registered (LineBreakingCallbacks) and reached, and the sweep's demand list asks for
  no break-align name at all -- so this is not a missing binding but the
  break-alignment machinery producing empty extents over columns that are still
  at zero, which is line-breaking/horizontal-spacing ground. It is the same
  "unplaced columns" family the ratchet fixes recorded for slashed-beam and
  could not guard.

WHAT THIS SAYS ABOUT THE TEN

  All ten regressed rows carry collapsed staff lines. The ten are one shape --
  the oracle writes one page, the port writes two, so the port's <base>-1.svg
  leaves the reference <base>.svg reading MISSING although the music drew --
  and they are sitting on a defect that touches 1,296 pages. Page breaking's
  close-out
  called them "not page-breaking bugs" and handed them to their real owners on
  the strength of per-file geometry; this session's suite-wide measurement is
  the stronger form of the same argument.

TESTS

  SpringStorageTests (3, Engine.Tests) fences the storage seam in BOTH
  directions: mutating a stored Spring through the List indexer must be
  DISCARDED, and through a span reference must be KEPT. The pair is the point --
  asserting only the ref case would pass against an indexer that started
  handing back references, and asserting only the copy case would pass against
  Spring quietly becoming a class. The third fences the alignment-distances
  trio together, because a spring that kept its minimum but lost its stretch
  strength would still drift.

  ⚠ OWED AND NOT WRITTEN: an end-to-end fence that alignment-distances actually
  places staves where a score asks. It is owed rather than skipped because the
  observable it needs -- vertical separation between two staves -- is carried in
  the SVG's enclosing translate transforms rather than in the line elements, and
  because every such measurement today runs over collapsed staff lines. It
  should be written once the finding above is closed, not before, or it fences
  broken geometry.

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE COLLAPSED-STAFF-LINE DEFECT, FIXED AT ITS ROOT
-------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  The page-breaking close-out measured it: 74.4% of the port's horizontal
  staff lines were
  drawn with no length (16,664 of 22,385 across 1,296 pages; oracle 4.9%).
  After this session: 1.4%, on 103 pages against the oracle's 145. All the
  fixes are faithful restorations; not one is new design.

  THE ROOT. Separation_item::boxes read an item's ORDINARY Y extent where
  upstream reads `il->pure_y_extent (ycommon, 0, INT_MAX)`. Boxes are built
  during horizontal spacing, BEFORE line breaking, and the ordinary read
  dragged the ordinary side-position chain in, which read the ordinary
  vertical-skylines, which asked the StaffSymbol for its STENCIL -- computed
  over columns still at x = 0, CACHED in the property alist, inherited by the
  broken piece through Clone, and DRAWN. Print never ran twice; the collapsed
  line was a stale cache, not a wrong computation.

  THE CLASS, SWEPT. Every "answers the ordinary extent, pending line
  breaking" note became a live bug the day line breaking landed, and nothing
  re-checked them -- the
  stale-named-absence rule at project scale. Retired this session (discharge
  notes inline at each record):

    * Separation_item::boxes                 pure_y_extent (the root)
    * Side_position_interface::aligned_side  support skylines, support Y
                                             coordinate, add-stem-support all
                                             maybe-pure; BOTH ReadSkylinePair
                                             constructor-default stand-ins are
                                             ordinary-branch only now
    * Accidental_placement::build_heads_skyline   pure_y_extent
    * Self_alignment_interface::aligned_on_self   maybe_pure_extent
    * Spacing_interface::skylines            pure_relative_y_coordinate
    * Paper_column::minimum_distance         the conditional_skyline merge

  THE DISCONNECTED MIDDLE. Paper_column_engraver was missing THREE
  acknowledgers (staff_spacing, note_spacing, break_alignment). Nothing ever
  wrote `spacing-wishes' onto any column, so every wish-based correction in
  Spacing_spanner has been DEAD since horizontal spacing landed -- reading
  empty sets, no
  diagnostic -- and no column carried a `break-alignment' object, so
  break_align_width answered a POINT at the column's coordinate for every
  staff-line end in the suite. (That is why close-out II's restored else
  branch "changed nothing": it searched an object no column carried.) With
  the wishes connected, two hollow interiors became reachable and were ported
  in full: Note_spacing::stem_dir_correction (knee_correction included) and
  Staff_spacing::optical_correction, both behind stale absence notes whose
  callees had long since landed.

  THE SPRING TRAP, THIRD INSTANCE. Page_layout_problem's
  alter_spring_from_spacing_spec took its Spring -- a struct -- BY VALUE, so
  no spacing spec ever reached any page spring: every system sat at the
  skyline MINIMUM instead of basic-distance (break.ly: 4.3 between systems
  where the oracle has 8.0; exact after the fix). Upstream passes Spring*;
  the port now passes `ref Spring'. Close-out II's List<Spring> fix was the
  same trap through an indexer; the ancient-notation Bezier was its mirror.
  The class was
  re-swept for by-value Spring parameters: NoteSpacing.GetSpacing is
  value-in/value-out like upstream, AppendSystem/AppendProb mutate-then-store,
  SpaceableGrob.AddSpring stores a copy upstream also stores. No other
  mutating by-value site remains.

  MEASURED, AGAINST THE COMMITTED FLOOR (nothing re-baselined):
    sweep 1 (root + side-position + acknowledgers): 9 regressed / 21 improved
      -- THE GATE WENT 10 -> 9 BY PORTING (script-custom-definition recovered)
